SBIO: Diversified Exposure To Biotech M&A Upside, Best Used As A Portfolio Diversifier

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

ALPS Medical Breakthroughs ETF can be used as a portfolio diversifier, providing exposure to 100 small- and mid-cap biotech companies in clinical trials.

SBIO seeks acquisition-driven upside while diversifying the risk of failed trials; its index requires at least 24 months of cash-burn coverage.

Rising rates, equity dilution, and slower M&A could weigh on SBIO; its 1.34 beta versus the NASDAQ Biotechnology Index signals substantial volatility.

The ALPS Medical Breakthroughs ETF (SBIO) is a passively managed exchange-traded fund designed to provide exposure to small- and mid-cap stocks of biotechnology companies with drugs in either Phase II or Phase III of US FDA clinical trials. With exposure to 100 biotech companies, SBIO provides diversified exposure to companies that may exhibit significant pharmaceutical breakthroughs that can result in substantial share price performance and a buyout from a major pharmaceutical company, offsetting the risk of failed trials. SBIO can be applied as a portfolio diversifier as a high-beta component of an asset allocation to the health care sector.

SBIO was launched by SS&C ALPS Funds on December 30, 2014 on the NYSE Arca Exchange. SBIO has a total expense ratio of 50 bps, comparable to peer sector-focused strategies on the market. At the time of writing, SBIO has $171 million in net assets with an average of $2.12 million in share value changing hands on a daily basis, exhibiting relatively thin liquidity for active traders. The ETF exhibits a relatively high bid/ask spread of 0.26%, adding to tradersâ round-trip costs.

SBIO was designed to track the performance of the VettaFi S-Network Medical Breakthroughs Index. The Index was designed to track the performance of small- and mid-cap biotechnology stocks listed on US stock exchanges that have one or more drugs in either Phase II or Phase III US FDA clinical trials. Companies must have a market capitalization of at least $200 million and no more than $5 billion for inclusion in the Index. In addition to this, constituents must be able to sustain their cash burn rates for at least 24 months. Any constituent exceeding the eligibility threshold by more than 10% will be removed from the Index. Constituents subject to a pending transaction and trading at net present value to the transaction will also be removed from the Index at the following quarterly rebalancing. The index weights constituents based on market capitalization, with no single constituent making up more than 4.5%.

SBIO provides exposure to 100 biotech companies spread across a variety of subsectors, including consumer non-cyclical at 29%, DREEN at 25%, and cancer at 21%. The top 10 holdings in the ETF account for 33% of the total portfolio weight. In contrast, the bottom 10 holdings amount to 0.41% of net assets. Top holdings currently include CG Oncology, Inc. (CGON) at 5.04%, Oruka Therapeutics, Inc. (ORKA) at 4.54%, and Veradermics, Incorporated (MANE) at 3.58%.

Given the structure of the Index, SBIO will be primarily allocated across pre-revenue companies undergoing clinical trials with the goal of successfully bringing a novel drug to market. The main growth driver for the portfolio is an announced acquisition by a major pharmaceutical company to add the drug finding to their platforms. M&A can deliver sporadic price performance for individual constituents in the portfolio as the company is expected to be acquired at a significant premium relative to the companyâs pre-revenue market capitalization.

Given the risks of actively selecting individual biotechnology stocks, the fund can provide investors with diversified exposure to capture the exposure to M&A over time while diversifying the risk of failed trials.

Given that these companies are pre-revenue, interest rates may be a critical component to valuing the present value of the companiesâ earnings potential from a drug finding. While the majority of these companies raise capital through equity issuances, a higher reference rate will require a higher expected rate of return to justify the companiesâ equity value. This means that pre-revenue biotechnology companies may underperform during periods of rising interest rates, which may impact the cost of capital and result in further share dilution.

With these risks in mind, investors may lean toward buying a portfolio of biotechnology stocks rather than actively selecting individual companies.

SBIO can be used by active and passive investors seeking exposure to the biotechnology market. Through diversified exposure, SBIO can provide upside potential in the event of an announced acquisition while diluting the risk of a failed trial. Given this factor, SBIO can be utilized for long-term exposure with the expectation of NAV growth.

Investors may use SBIO for active exposure, though the State Street SPDR S&P Biotech ETF (XBI) will provide greater liquidity with $9.4b in net assets and a lower management fee of 35 bps. Given that biotechnology stocks may be valued based on macroeconomic factors such as announced funding programs and interest rates, active traders can trade the ETF based on their expected market environment.

For long-term exposure, SBIO could be used as a portfolio diversifier as the ETF may bear significant volatility risk throughout market cycles. SBIO exhibits a beta risk of 1.34x relative to the NASDAQ Biotechnology Index and can expose investors to aggressive price movements in the short term.

The biotechnology sector can bear significant risks that must be considered prior to making a final investment decision. The fund is primarily allocated to pre-revenue companies that may expose investors to the risk of failed trials. If M&A activity were to slow down, SBIO may exhibit limited upside potential from acquisitions to offset value decay. Biotechnology companies largely finance operations through equity raises and may deeply dilute shareholder value during drug trials.

SBIO can provide investors with diversified exposure to small- and mid-cap biotechnology companies undergoing drug trials. Through diversification, SBIO should provide investors with broad coverage of companies that may successfully undergo drug trials, offsetting the risk of failed trials. SBIO can be best utilized as a portfolio diversifier as a component of pharmaceutical exposure.

Is Annaly Capital Stock a Buy, Sell, or Hold About 25% Below Its 52-Week High?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Annaly's slumping share price has driven up its dividend yield.

By Matt DiLallo – Nov 25, 2026 at 8:45AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Annaly Capital Management ›

Shares of Annaly Capital Management (NLY +2.33%) have tumbled more than 25% from their 52-week high. That has the mortgage-focused real estate investment trust (REIT) trading at a dividend yield of more than 16.5%.

That seems like an enticing yield for income-seeking investors. Here's a look at whether the high-dividend REIT is a buy, sell, or hold at its currently lower price point.

The main factor weighing on Annaly's stock is rising interest rates. The 10-year Treasury rate has risen from a low of 4% to around 5% over the past few months. When rates rise, the value of fixed-income investments like the mortgage-backed securities Annaly holds falls. That's evident in the decline in Annaly's book value, which fell from $20.15 per share in the second quarter to $17.86 per share in the third quarter.

An investment in Annaly at this point is a bet on two things. First, that it can generate enough earnings available for distribution (EAD) to continue paying its high-yielding dividend, which the REIT did during the third quarter, as EAD of $0.80 per share comfortably covered its $0.75 per share dividend. The improvement in EAD in recent years has enabled Annaly to increase its dividend twice. However, that followed a string of cuts in prior years.

The other factor you're betting on is a relatively stable interest rate environment. If rates surge, Annaly's MBS portfolio would lose value (and its borrowing costs would rise). However, while falling rates would boost the value of its holdings (and lower costs), they would also reduce the yield on new MBS investments, which could affect returns.

While Annaly could deliver a strong total return from its current price point, I believe the long-term return will likely be much lower than its current yield implies. As its history shows, the company has delivered a rather pedestrian total return since its IPO, even with dividends reinvested. That's why I sold my shares a few years ago and wouldn't buy more now, even though they're 25% below the 52-week high.

Trump is proposing new tariffs on China, the E.U., and 12 other economies

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The proposed Section 301 duties target industrial overcapacity, with China facing a rate that could rise to 40%

The Trump administration proposed new tariffs on China, the European Union and 12 other economies on Wednesday, arguing that the countries singled out maintain more factory capacity than they need and that this weighs on U.S. business. The 14 economies received advance notice of the actions.

The proposal covers Bangladesh, Cambodia, China, the EU, India, Indonesia, Japan, South Korea, Malaysia, Singapore, Switzerland, Taiwan, Thailand and Vietnam, according to Bloomberg.

China would face a 20% rate in addition to the 25% duty that took effect in 2018, according to CNBC. That rate could climb to 40% if Beijing does not honor its commitments under the trade truce reached in October 2025 in Busan, South Korea.

Goods from the EU, Japan, South Korea and Switzerland would face 15% rates that could rise to 20%, according to Bloomberg. Indian goods would be taxed at 17% if New Delhi seals a trade agreement with Washington, and at up to 25% under the proposal.

The new duties would not be piled onto the forced-labor tariffs of 10% or 12.5%. For goods that qualify under both, the larger of the two rates would be charged, according to Bloomberg. Oil and gas, food the U.S. cannot grow domestically, and goods already hit by sector-specific duties, such as pharmaceuticals and critical minerals, would be exempt.

The proposal will go through a period of public input and could be revised. A final determination might land in January at the earliest, according to Bloomberg. Comparable steps targeting Mexico and Norway were omitted from the proposal, though officials are still weighing them.

China's foreign ministry said on Wednesday that "the so-called 'overcapacity' does not really exist," according to CNBC. The trade truce between Washington and Beijing runs out in January unless both sides renew it, according to Bloomberg.

The administration had been preparing to raise duties on the targeted economies after a Section 301 investigation that the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative opened in March. U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer has said state backing has left those economies with more industrial capacity than they need, which lets their companies undercut competitors abroad on price and hurts American manufacturers.

Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974 gives Washington a way to retaliate against overseas commercial conduct it judges unfair or a burden on U.S. commerce. In February, the Supreme Court invalidated the duties President Donald Trump had levied under the International Emergency Economic Powers Act, according to CNBC.

After the ruling, Trump invoked Section 122 of the Trade Act to levy a worldwide tariff of 10%, according to CNBC. He later swapped that tariff for duties of up to 12.5% covering 60 economies, imposed through Section 301 on the grounds of forced-labor practices. Those duties face legal challenges, according to Bloomberg.

Christie’s acquires British auction house Dreweatts

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Dreweatts will continue to operate from its Donnington Priory salesroom in Newbury, Berkshire

Christie's announced today it has acquired the British auction house Dreweatts, a move that confirms the enduring appeal of antiques and older collectibles, particularly as profit margins in other parts of the auction sector remain squeezed.

Both businesses will run as before, Christie’s confirms, with no staffing changes expected as a result of the deal. Drewatts will retain its name and continue to be run “independently” by its chief executive, Henry House, and chairman Will Richards, said Christie’s president, Europe, Middle East and Africa Anthea Peers in a statement. Dreweatts will continue to operate from its salesroom at Donnington Priory in Newbury, Berkshire, with no sales expected to move to London.

Drewatts, founded in 1759, is best known for country house and estate sales, and its specialist departments covering furniture, Old Masters, ceramics, jewellery, silver, clocks and wine. It holds around 70 sales a year. In 2025, it posted a record total auction turnover of £37.56m (£47.3m with fees)—a 26% increase from its 2024 results. Both Christie’s and Dreweatts are privately owned and do not disclose their profits.

A display ahead of the sale of Patrick and Amabel Lindsay’s collection at Dreweatts

The acquisition effectively sees Christie’s backtrack its 2017 decision to close its South Kensington venue specialised in antiques, known as Christie's South Kensington or CSK. This decision was taken as the market for postwar and contemporary art was at its peak, and as the trade chased global expansion and billionaire tastes at the expense of localism and conoisseurship.

Today, those sales of Modern and postwar trophy works might generate records and headlines, but the overheads for winning these prize consignments devours profit margins. Indeed, many leading industry figures criticised Christie’s decision to close CSK as a shortsighted one that haemorrhaged its strongholds of specialist knowledge and staff.

Christie's president Bonnie Brennan says in a statement that Dreweatts possesses “particular strength in a part of the market that is highly complementary to our own. Most importantly, our clients collect across categories and at every price point, and together we have the opportunity to provide them with an even broader and more complete level of service.”

This marks the second major acquisition of a smaller auction house by Christie’s in recent years, following the 2024 buyout of the classic car vendors Gooding & Company.

Christie’s acquires Dreweatts from the advisory firm Gurr Johns, which bought the regional auction house in 2017 for £1.25m, as The Art Newspaper reported at the time. “I believe that Christie’s is the only business with the capacity, experience and shared culture to fulfil the further potential of Dreweatts,”, Gurr Johns chief told the Financial Times.

The house's auction total saw a double-digit decrease for a second year in a row—but private sales are booming

Gooding & Company was founded in 2003 by a former Christie’s director

The two rivals have made record online and private sales as pandemic overturns traditional live auction format

Core inflation beat forecasts in October, reinforcing expectations of a December Fed rate hike

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The overall PCE price index rose 3.8% from a year earlier, matching forecasts, ahead of the Fed's Dec. 8-9 meeting

The Bureau of Economic Analysis reported on Wednesday that the core personal consumption expenditures price index, which excludes food and energy, rose 3.3% in October from a year earlier. That topped the Dow Jones consensus estimate by a tenth of a percentage point, according to CNBC.

The overall PCE price index rose 3.8% from a year earlier, up from 3.6% in September. That matched economists' expectations, according to CNBC.

From September, both the overall index and the core index rose 0.3%. Personal income rose 0.7% in October and consumer spending rose 0.3%. Real spending fell $5.8 billion, and the personal saving rate was 4.1%.

The Commerce Department also revised third-quarter GDP growth to a 4.3% annualized rate on Wednesday, according to CNBC.

The inflation data reinforced expectations of another rate hike in December, according to CNBC. The Federal Reserve meets Dec. 8-9. Market odds of a quarter-point increase at that meeting stand at about 97% on CME $CME Group's FedWatch Tool, according to ABC News. A December hike would be the Fed's second in four months.

Dallas Fed President Lorie Logan said earlier this month that the central bank needs at least two more quarter-point rate increases. She said core PCE inflation was 3.1% over the 12 months through September. The Fed held its target range at 3.75% to 4% at its October meeting.

Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech before the October consumer price index release that the Fed should consider a half-point increase in December if core inflation printed at 0.3% or above. He said a quarter-point move in that case would put the Fed's credibility at risk. Core CPI rose 0.3% in October, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics.

‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ Made Nearly $4 Billion Doing What Movies Can’t

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“Grand Theft Auto VI” took less than 24 hours to generate $3.79 billion in global sales. That’s roughly 15 percent of the year’s worldwide box office — in under a day.

But Hollywood’s biggest concern about games might not be what else consumers are spending their money on. It could be just how many days, weeks, or even months people end up playing “GTA VI” instead of streaming movies or TV on other platforms.

Even before Rockstar’s record-breaking launch on November 19, entertainment news outlets like TheWrap were asking whether the game studio’s historic release would seriously disrupt the attention economy. Several analysts echoed that concern in their own industry assessments, warning investors that the sprawling open-world adventure could monopolize living-room airspace until well after the holidays. Related Stories Frederick Wiseman’s Complete Filmography to Be Distributed by Janus Films Movie Theaters in 2026: Filmmakers and Exhibitors on What’s Good, What’s Bad, and What’s Changing

There’s a sort of delicious irony in realizing a game named after a felony has Hollywood panicking about stolen screen time. Personally speaking, as a former video game critic who now mostly covers film and television, I’m fascinated by the competition for both audience engagement and consumer interest.

Still, that particular side of “GTA VI” discourse drives me up the wall. Games, movies, TV shows, and social media feeds do indeed battle for our attention, and the business of entertainment is ultimately all about influencing how strangers use their free time.

Rather, the issue lies in the assumption that one hour spent with one type of story is automatically equivalent to another hour spent with an entirely different medium — and that comparing those numbers tells us a thing about what either is really worth.

Last Thursday, I was at my favorite bar, catching up with a friend visiting from Seattle, when she got up to greet someone else. Scrolling on my phone, I opened a DM from my old coworker that said he was… already playing “GTA VI”? That couldn’t be!

Confused, I quickly searched “GTA 6 release date” and discovered I wasn’t just a few hours away from entering Vice City — but that I was already a full day behind the rest of the gaming world. Yes, Rockstar had spent more than a decade preparing its masterpiece, and I had somehow missed the actual drop like a tricky freeway exit.

I really should’ve known better, too. I’d preordered “GTA VI” and downloaded it on my PlayStation days earlier, but even $79.99 deep, I was caught off guard by the sudden arrival of Lucia and Jason, who, even welcome, had seriously complicated my night.

Never one to miss a groundbreaking moment in a fake place, I ran home to my apartment, determined to make up for lost time using whatever post-bar energy I had. (As it turns out, that wasn’t much.)

There’s something weirdly prudish about Hollywood’s anxiety over just how much time “GTA VI” fans might spend playing it — as though immersive and interactive narratives were an indulgence taking hours away from more legitimate entertainment. Games have been a major market and creative stage for decades, and still, the conversation around Rockstar’s new blockbuster brings to mind the sort of worried parents who would’ve been afraid of TV in the ’90s.

Those pearl-clutching concerns also miss a practical distinction. Playing a video game demands a different kind of physical and mental engagement than watching a movie or TV show. Admittedly, the first few minutes of “GTA VI” make that distinction remarkably easy to forget — opening on Lucia in prison for a sequence so beautifully lit and photographed that interacting with inmates feels at once totally real and like Rockstar showing off.

And yet, even in the “cinematic” wonder of that moment, rushing home left me in no state to steer a virtual boat. My first real “GTA VI” mission involved navigating choppy waters to pick up (and/or drop off, I honestly cannot remember) bricks of cocaine, but unfortunately, bro, I could not operate heavy machinery.

What felt like hours of circling checkpoints was probably closer to 15 minutes. Still, the technical wizardry bobbing across my screen clashed with my stomach contents, and I was desperate to stop. So, I turned off my PS5 and streamed “Knocked Up,” instead.

I woke up to the sound of Katherine Heigl crowning and went to bed at 1 a.m., truly grateful that Judd Apatow’s 2007 comedy had freed me from the obligation to captain anything. But that night, sober as a judge and eager to get behind the wheel again, I remembered what the decade-plus of “GTA VI” hype was all about.

My first in-game police pursuit was astounding in its intricacy, with Leonida (i.e., Rockstar’s Florida) providing a jaw-dropping menagerie of local characters, each living out a personal drama or comedy of errors against a backdrop of boundless scenery.

Weaving through traffic and cutting down alleys, I realized I wanted the full “Fast and Furious” feeling early and often. You know, tightly choreographed fever dreams complete with stunt drivers, cinematographers, and someone other than me to call cut. But here, all I had was myself: a shitty chauffeur, a worse debut director, and a leading man who kept mistakenly jumping onto the roof of her car before getting in.

To be fair, “GTA VI” offers a truly impressive range of camera options when you decide to properly educate yourself on the system’s customizable controls. But as a woman who couldn’t keep her wheels on the road, let alone capture Jason’s stubble from a flattering angle, I was practically begging for a union rep — and a body double. Worse still, I was getting more and more worried about my girl, Lucia.

Rockstar had sold me on Jason as a good boyfriend, and I wanted to prioritize that interpretation in my playthrough. But the longer I waited for Lucia to get out of prison, the more convinced I became that she might never be coming back. Had I missed a mission? Decapitated the wrong innocent bystander? Whatever had gone wrong in my game, uncertainty powered Jason’s decline.

I robbed a woman after she refused my offer to help with her broken-down car. I took surprising pleasure in dispatching adversarial gang members. After a seemingly infinite number of crashes, I cared less about playing the romantic lead than about combining my growing enthusiasm for scuba diving with organized crime.

In a movie, that sort of moral deterioration might suggest a carefully constructed character arc. In “GTA VI,” it mostly reflected my own impatience. Rockstar had given me the raw materials for a compelling love story, but the longer I waited for Lucia, the more willfully criminal my version of Jason became. By Friday night, I was having a good time — but I was also getting critically hardened, and increasingly convinced I’d done something wrong to derail the romance I craved.

Around 3 a.m. on Saturday, I was throwing up into my toilet with the word “WASTED” practically hovering above my head in enormous white letters. My plans to spend the weekend robbing old ladies and getting gradually pissier with the absent Lucia had been derailed by a profoundly gross bout of what seemed to be food poisoning.

Yeah, it was a real bummer, dog. But unable to muster the constitution for a car ride, scuba diving, or the next step in my budding drug empire, I settled for fixing up Jason’s rickety house and lifting weights on his back deck. Nauseous, I even briefly considered finishing Lucia’s bedroom, but gave up after getting stuck picking just one color from a trio of offscreen paint samples. (I didn’t know her well enough to know which one she’d like, and fighting a fever, that felt like an argument in the making.)

There was something genuinely lovely about occupying Rockstar’s beautifully realized environments without any particular objective in mind. Even under the weather, I understood why I’d seen so many people on social media comparing “GTA VI” to “slow cinema,” a style of filmmaking that finds meaning in naturalistic observation and drawn-out routines.

Its more intimate moments sometimes reminded me of Sean Baker’s “The Florida Project,” too, with its colorful vision of the Sunshine State smashing together the faded appeal of amusement parks and painful hallmarks of everyday desperation.

That’s a lot to contend with when you’re sick, though, so I turned on “The Hunting Wives” (another gloriously lurid piece of modern American satire!) and, while scrolling through social media, I was struck by a contrast.

Eight episodes of “The Hunting Wives” could simply wash over me. To get the same experience from “GTA VI,” I’d have to hunt through Vice City and its vast collection of in-universe media myself. Both had plenty to say about contemporary America. Only one required me to do much of anything to hear it.

So I was genuinely surprised when I began encountering livestreams on TikTok of people playing through later stages of the “GTA VI” story, including a scooter-stealing mission I’d just discovered but hadn’t tried yet. Then I caught a glimpse of someone playing as… Lucia?!

I scrolled away immediately. Having spent Friday convinced I’d sabotaged the romance through my own choices, I was relieved to see I was still on track, but simultaneously annoyed that someone else’s playthrough had spoiled part of my plot.

Still, I couldn’t stop laughing at the online debate over whether “GTA VI” was too woke, or whether Rockstar had successfully tricked millions of players into simulating healthy relationships. I’d bought the game partly because I wanted to participate in a massive cultural moment, and here I was, too sick to play but still in the thick of things — watching it unfold through Netflix, TikTok, and strangers whose versions of Vice City were already far ahead of mine.

It was exciting, irritating, occasionally informative, and even without my controller, I found myself making decisions about how I wanted to play later. (One person, for example, painted Lucia’s bedroom peach, a choice I disliked so much that I made both a mental and literal note to go with green in the end.)

By Sunday night, I was finally feeling well enough to enjoy the entertainment I’d been trying to consume all weekend. Lucia had come home in my playthrough, and exploring their relationship together made “GTA VI” click in a way no amount of aimless driving could.

At a lavish yacht party, interacting with other guests became an opportunity to figure out what sort of couple these two actually were. Were Jason and Lucia the kind of boyfriend and girlfriend who did drugs together? Would romantic jealousy be an issue, or were we cool with [wink] that?

Suddenly, I wasn’t waiting for Rockstar to tell me what their connection meant. I was actively deciding, and having thoroughly mastered the mechanics of walking around without looking like a complete lunatic by then, I was putting some swag into it.

A particularly sexy jet-skiing sequence made an impression, with Jason holding onto Lucia as she drove instead of the other way around. It was a sweet little reversal of gender norms, and the kind of affectionate character detail I’d been waiting to discover. Even better, it was happening in a game that seemed to understand exactly how much I wanted to drive its love story.

Then came the Carly Rae Jepsen car chase.

Glossy and expensive, our borrowed vehicle reflected the neon lights of Vice City like a scene from “Blade Runner.” With 2015 chart-topper “Run Away with Me” blasting from the speakers, Lucia and Jason were living out the spectacular criminal romance I’d been hoping for. Skyscrapers towered around our blossoming love, and I was riding high…

… Until I smashed us into a cement divider and Jason’s head exploded.

As the game reset and made my sudden brush with vehicular homicide a thing of the past, I found myself laughing like gunfire alone in my apartment. I was suddenly orchestrating a crime of continuity no movie could ever get away with — and it was one of my favorite “GTA VI” moments yet.

Great movies and TV shows often succeed by creating characters who sincerely feel like real people. Rockstar’s new release is exceptionally good at that. But an equally extraordinary connection between art and its audience can emerge from acknowledging that the people experiencing your story have their own needs, limitations, and personalities, too.

That’s the fundamental difference between this extraordinary simulator and a fantastic film. At its best, cinema invites you to inhabit a vision so singular it could only belong to someone else. A great video game gives you the tools to make someone else’s vision singularly your own. That takes a degree of commitment that doesn’t always accommodate the realities of being a person, like, oh say, racing to the bathroom with your digestive system in active, contaminant-born revolt.

Even so, when I think about the hours I spent with “GTA VI” this weekend, I’m not particularly interested in how much other “content” I could have been watching instead. No filmmaker could have anticipated the precise emotional satisfaction I’d get from that magenta-hued highway catastrophe, and, purely from a practical standpoint, no self-respecting artist should have to.

Rockstar built Vice City over the span of a decade, but that spectacularly stupid scene in all its accidental skull-smashing glory was mine — proof there’s a world of difference between commanding someone’s attention and handing them the keys.

Real Estate Tycoon Mauricio Umansky Reveals What Luxury Buyers Want Most—From Secluded Estates to Private Spas

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Umansky has a read on what makes a multimillion-dollar home irresistible.

For some of his buyers, it’s enough acreage to put real distance between them and the neighbors. For others, it’s the luxury of stepping into a sauna or taking a cold plunge without leaving home.

“Privacy is becoming a bigger priority for luxury buyers,” The Agency founder revealed while speaking to Mansion Global.

He said buyers are drawn to properties where they can retreat from view, feel secure and build a wellness routine around amenities such as a private gym or spa.

Umansky is putting his read on the market to work in Miami’s Edgewater neighborhood, where he is leading sales for HQ Residences Miami.

The project brings together singer Marc Anthony, hospitality entrepreneur Sam Nazarian and SBE. Its pitch borrows from the hotel world, pairing private residences with wellness amenities, places for neighbors to gather and an emphasis on service.

Even at the top of the market, buyers are watching what they pay. Umansky said there’s room to negotiate in some areas, while a well-priced home in a coveted location can still draw strong interest.

He recommends finding an agent who knows the neighborhood and being ready to act when a property comes along that you can see yourself living in for years.

Umansky’s own homes span Los Angeles, La Quinta, California, and Aspen, Colorado.

Los Angeles gives him access to city life, while La Quinta is where he sees the appeal of a slower pace, sunny days and a round of golf. In Aspen, the draw extends from ski season to summers in the mountains and the sense of community he finds there.

If he were to add another address, Umansky said he could picture himself in Europe.

He singled out the South of France, Italy and Spain, with particular affection for Madrid. Miami also appeals to him as a place to own a home, especially after spending more time in the city through his work on HQ Residences.

The broker also reflected on what television has done for his business. Appearances on “Buying Beverly Hills,” “Dancing With the Stars” and “Special Forces” have introduced him to people he might never have met through real estate alone, he said, and brought more attention to The Agency.

The trade-off is that some people form an impression of him from what they see on camera. He said he wants his reputation to rest on his work as an agent and the trust he earns from clients.

As for the prospect of running for mayor, Umansky spoke about what he would focus on if he pursued the job.

“Los Angeles is my home, and I care deeply about its future,” he told Mansion Global. He said he’d look at how the city handles housing approvals and development, with the goal of making it easier to build and attracting more investment and jobs.

The candid glimpse into what wealthy buyers are looking for comes one week after his stepdaughter and, Kyle Richards' daughter, Farrah Aldjufrie, gave birth to a baby boy—three months after revealing the identity of her unborn child's father as none other than her "Buying Beverly Hills" co-star and The Agency colleague, Zach Goldsmith.

The 37-year-old property mogul, who is the daughter of Richards and her ex-husband, Guraish Aldjufrie, shared the news in July that she was expecting her first child.

Then, one month later, she hard launched her relationship with Goldsmith while sharing a video of the two of them celebrating their gender reveal.

Then, a source told Page Six that the TV alum has welcomed her son; however, Aldjufrie has yet to confirm the news herself.

The pregnancy marks a joyful personal milestone for the real estate mogul, whose professional achievements have often played out in front of TV audiences.

Aldjufrie has previously revealed how Umansky helped to cultivate her love of real estate, explaining in a 2016 interview with The Daily Dish that she used to drive around to different listed properties in L.A. with her stepfather as a child and was fascinated by everything that went into selling them.

“I always have been interested in real estate since I was a little kid. I’d drive around to open houses with my mom and Mauricio when I was 6, 7, 8 years old. I’d love to see all the houses,” she said.

“I know L.A. geographically really, really well, having grown up here. I was an only child for the first eight years, so I spent a lot of time with them and was very interested in everything Mauricio was doing. But I have my own love for property and being in L.A., there's some of the most amazing properties and lifestyles in the world.”

According to her profile on The Agency's website, Aldjufrie first became an agent in 2013—and has since helped the company to achieve "over $5 billion in real estate sales."

Interestingly, while she continues her professional and personal relationship with The Agency's founder, Umansky, her mother made clear just a few months ago that she believes she is entitled to a significant chunk of her estranged husband's empire—telling her Bravo co-stars that she will fight to claim her half of the business that she says she helped to build should the couple end up filing for divorce.

Umansky founded The Agency alongside Billy Rose and Blair Chang in 2011, 15 years after he wed Richards—and by May 2022, the business had facilitated more than $57 billion worth of real estate sales, according to Forbes.

According to The Agency's website, the company currently operates more than 145 offices in 14 countries, while Umansky's bio notes that he has "achieved nearly $5 billion in real estate sales and holds the distinction of selling the most homes in the country priced above $20 million.

"He has represented some of the world's most noteworthy properties, including the Playboy Mansion—the first house in L.A. to sell above the $100 million mark—the Walt Disney Estate, and residences owned by Michael Jackson, Michael Jordan and Prince."

Indeed, in the 15 years since it was founded, The Agency has built up a wealth of clients, working with the likes of Lady Gaga, LeBron James, and Ben Affleck.

While it's unclear whether Richards would seek to be involved in the day-to-day running of the business that she hopes to claim a 50% share in, it's likely that she could be in line for a hefty payout if Umansky wishes to keep The Agency to himself.

However, Richards noted that she isn't quite ready to pull the plug on their marriage once and for all.

She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Danny DeVito-Produced SXSW Heist Comedy ‘Drag’ Scores Wave of International Sales Out of AFM (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“Drag,” the SXSW heist comedy starring Lizzy Caplan and John Stamos and produced by Danny DeVito, has secured a run of international deals following the American Film Market.

U.K. sales and distribution company Blue Finch Films, which handles the title, has closed Germany with Indeed Film, France with VOD Factory and Spain with A Contracorriente. Poland went to 9th Plan, China to JL Vision and the Middle East and North Africa to Falcon Films, while Penny Black took airline rights. Blue Finch will handle the release itself in the U.K. and Ireland. In North America, Briarcliff Entertainment holds rights and will open the film in theaters on Jan. 29, 2027.

Fed’s preferred inflation gauge accelerated to 3.8% in October, likely sealing rate hike

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Inflation moved further away from the Federal Reserve's target in October, reinforcing expectations that another interest rate hike is in cards for December and raising questions about how much more work the central bank will have to do in 2027.

The personal consumption expenditures price index, the Fed's preferred inflation gauge, showed a seasonally adjusted 3.8% annual inflation rate, in line with the Dow Jones consensus estimate and nearly twice the central bank's goal.

Excluding food and energy, core PCE was 3.3%, the Commerce Department reported Wednesday. That was 0.1 percentage point above the forecast.

While the Fed officially follows the headline figure, policymakers consider core to be a more reliable longer-term gauge of inflation trends.

A separate report also showed much stronger economic growth for the third quarter, with gross domestic product rising at a 4.3% annualized rate, according to a revised Commerce Department estimate. The strong top-line gain for the economy adds further fuel to the notion that Fed monetary policy is not restricting economic activity.

Consumer spending rose 0.3% for the month, which matched the pace of price increases but was 0.1 percentage point lower than forecast. Income, on the other hand, surged 0.7%, much more than the 0.4% estimate. The personal saving rate rose to 4.1%.

The report comes with the market fully pricing in a quarter percentage point interest rate hike when the Fed meets Dec. 8-9. However, it leaves unanswered how much more rates will need to increase to get inflation back down to the Fed's 2% target. Inflation has been running above target for some 5½ years.

The large upward increase in GDP came from revisions to investment as well as consumer and government spending.

Rams vs. Packers Thanksgiving Eve halftime show to feature U2: How to watch

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

They once filled your iPhone with an album. Now they're filling up Thanksgiving Eve and doing it "With or Without You."

U2 is set to headline the halftime show for the NFL's first-ever game on "Thanksgiving Eve," a term coined by the league for this event. The Irish rock band will take the stage when the Los Angeles Rams and Green Bay Packers head for a break at SoFi Stadium in Los Angeles.

It marks a return to the spotlight for the group, who is fresh off the release of their new album "Carnaval De Luz" on Nov. 13.

MORE: What NFL games are on Thanksgiving? Teams, matchups, TV channels for every game

As if that wasn't enough, U2 also just revealed the North America legs of "The Greatest Show on Earth World Tour," meaning the NFL's holiday game is also somewhat of a promo for U2's 50th anniversary.

'Twas the night before Thanksgiving, when U2 will be singing.

Here's what to know about the NFL's halftime show on the night before Turkey Day.

Detroit Lions players celebrate their 23-20 win against the Chicago Bears with a turkey drumstick at Ford Field on Nov. 28, 2024. The celebration was made possible by some highly questionable clock management by Bears head coach Matt Eberflus. A day later, Eberflus was fired by the Bears, the first in-season firing of a head coach in franchise history.

Dallas Cowboys running back Ezekiel Elliott poses with the Galloping Gobbler trophy after the win over Washington at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas on Nov 24, 2016. The Cowboys won the game, 31-26. Dak Prescott and Ezekiel Elliott put on an offensive showcase.

During a Thanksgiving Day game in 2013, Pittsburgh Steelers head coach Mike Tomlin got into the action. The Baltimore Ravens' Jacoby Jones broke free on a kickoff return, but Tomlin stepped onto the field, causing Jones to avoid the Steelers coach along the sideline. Instead of a touchdown, Jones was tackled after a 73-yard return. The Ravens went on to win the game, 22-20.

New York Jets quarterback Mark Sanchez collides with the backside of lineman Brandon Moore and fumbles the ball. It was recovered and returned for a touchdown by the New England Patriots' Steve Gregory. The play was part of a disastrous sequence for the Jets, who lost three fumbles as the Patriots scored three touchdowns in 52 seconds. New England went on to win 49-19, and Sanchez's infamous moment from the 2012 game is known as "The Butt Fumble."

Dallas Cowboys quarterback Tony Romo celebrates a touchdown against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers in the third quarter NFL action in Irving, Texas Nov. 23, 2006. In a breakout game, Romo threw for 306 yards and five TDs.

On his way to 49 touchdown passes for the season, Peyton Manning put six on the board in the Indianapolis Colts' masterful 41-9 win over the Detroit Lions. Manning went 23-of-28 for 236 yards and six touchdowns before being pulled near the end of third quarter in 2004.

Randy Moss added another tally to his incredible rookie year in in the Minnesota Vikings' 46-36 win over the Dallas Cowboys, catching three touchdown passes for 163 yards in 1998.

With the Detroit Lions and Pittsburgh Steelers headed to overtime, referee Phil Luckett's microphone caught Pittsburgh captain Jerome Bettis calling "tails" on the coin toss. Despite the toss coming up tails, Luckett claimed Detroit won the toss. The Lions opted to receive the kickoff and scored a field goal on the first possession to win the game in 1998.

Hall of Famer Barry Sanders ran for 167 yards and three touchdowns as the Detroit Lions obliterated the Chicago Bears, 55-20, in 1997. Sanders moved into second place on the NFL’s all-time rushing yardage list during the game.

The Dallas Cowboys' Leon Lett dives for a blocked fourth-quarter field goal attempt as Bert Weidner of the Miami Dolphins scrambles for the ball on Nov. 25, 1993, in Irving, Texas. The live ball was recovered by Jeff Dellenbach of the Dolphins. Miami kicker Pete Stoyanovich then made the winning 19-yard field goal as time expired giving the Dolphins a 16-14 victory.

Buffalo Bills running back O.J. Simpson rushed for an NFL record 273 yards against the Detroit Lions on Thanksgiving, Nov 25, 1976 in Pontiac, Michigan.

U2 will be the halftime entertainment during the Rams' Thanksgiving Eve game against the Packers.

Not only is it the NFL's first game on the night before Thanksgiving, but it also represents somewhat of a kickoff to U2's upcoming world tour that begins in Feb. 2027.

Netflix co-CEO Ted Sarandos believes the combination of football and U2 will make for an "unforgettable night."

"There's a reason U2 has headlined stadiums for decades: They don't just perform; they deliver iconic moments that audiences remember forever," Sarandos said. "We're thrilled that they'll bring that same magic to the NFL stage on Netflix. It's going to be an unforgettable night for football fans and Netflix members worldwide."

Notably, this will not be U2's first NFL halftime show. They previously took the stage for the Super Bowl 36 halftime show at the Superdome in New Orleans on Feb. 3, 2002.

One of the more memorable halftime shows in Super Bowl history, U2 paid tribute to the victims of the Sept. 11, 2001, terrorist attacks, projecting their names while performing. Bono later revealed an American flag in his jacket lining.

Formed in Dublin in 1976, the group has withstood the test of time. Comprised of Bono, the Edge, Adam Clayton and Larry Mullen Jr., U2 has won 22 Grammy Awards in addition to being inducted into the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame.

They just released their 16th studio album, "Carnaval De Luz," marking the group's first in nine years that has new material.

The album released coincided with the announcement of a new world tour that begins in 2027, their first stadium tour since 2019.

U2's halftime show won't begin until the end of the second quarter. Those looking to tune in for just the show should check-in around 9 p.m. ET, just in case it's a fast-moving first half.

The show will likely begin around 9:30 p.m. ET.

It will be broadcasted exclusively on Netflix, unless you live in the local market of the Packers or Rams. Here's a look at the channels and ways to watch U2 perform:

TV channel Los Angeles (local only): KNBC

TV channel Green Bay (local only): WGBA (NBC 26)

TV channel Milwaukee (local only): WTMJ (TMJ4)

British Industry Braces For Impact Of America’s Federal Film & TV Tax Incentive

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Steven Paul may think the proposed federal film and TV tax incentive is “good for the world,” but the British industry isn’t so sure.

UK trade bodies, industry organizations and studios are peering nervously toward the other side of the pond at the incentive’s progress, which has now received the full-throated backing of Donald Trump. That is because American studios and streamers have made huge productions in the UK for years including the likes of Barbie, Wicked and Amazon’s Lord of the Rings, generating billions in local production revenue and creating tens of thousands of jobs. Related Stories News UK Culture Minister Delivers Emotional Plea To YouTube To "Set An Example" & Remove Russell Brand's Channel News BFI & Chanel Filmmaker Award: 'Ish' Director Imran Perretta Among 2026 Winners

Deadline understands the British Film Institute (BFI) has formed a working group with other industry bodies and bigwigs to probe the incentive’s potential impact on the sector and consider what levers can be pulled to nullify this impact. Watch on Deadline

The working group was assembled recently and is not only tackling the tax incentive but wider macro issues hitting the film and TV industry such as the slowdown in commissioning, falling budgets, job cuts and sharper competition from tax credits in other nations and U.S. states. “A broad group of screen sector organisations have come together to identify measures which will ensure the UK remains a world-leading home for domestic and international production,” said a BFI spokeswoman. “This is looking at a range of challenging conditions facing the sector, including increased international competitiveness.”

The fear is that the attractiveness of the proposed 20% American credit — Trump has urged lawmakers to pass the “amazing” bipartisan bill — and broader drive to bring production back to the U.S. will make studios think twice before setting up projects in the UK. While there is clearly political will in the U.S. to force the measure through after years of losing local production, possibly before the current lame-duck congress session concludes, UK industry sources cautioned that there is no sign of productions relocating today. Big projects filming in Q4 2026 and next year include new seasons of Mobland and House of the Dragon, along with Guy Ritchie’s Dogfight for Netflix.

Potential levers could include improving the UK’s own 25% tax credit, generating more funding from elsewhere or improving local production. Improving the UK tax credit, which has been a huge success story since coming in more than a decade ago, has been discussed for many years but the ruling Labour Party has shown little inclination to play ball.

“There are levers and we need to discuss them,” said one industry source familar with the working group. “This isn’t just about raising [our own] tax credits. We need to improve the domestic situation.”

The UK government has now weighed in publicly.

In what felt a deliberate and timely intervention, culture minister Ian Murray told last week’s British Screen Forum conference his team “takes the implications [of the credit] incredibly seriously.” Without elaborating, Murray said the outcome “remains to be seen.”

Murray’s team declined further comment on what actions the government is taking but sources have welcomed his remarks as proof the government is cognisant of the issue. “Ian was showing the industry he has their back,” said the industry source quoted above.

At the same event, Aardman boss Sean Clarke described the tax credit as a “real issue” for the industry. This is because inward investment, most of it from Americans, has for years made up the bulk of Britain’s film and TV revenue. But amid a worldwide commissioning slowdown, this has been slipping. Recent BFI figures showed that spend on film and high-end TV plummeted by 28% for the year to September 30. The figures are “the result of a complex picture, ranging from significant shifts in the global industrial context through to increased competitiveness from other international territories, and set against a backdrop of a strong Q1-Q3 period in 2025,” the BFI spokeswoman said, pointing to huge projects that filmed in 2025 like Avengers: Doomsday, Spider-Man: Brand New Day and the Harry Potter TV series.

The UK, with its lucrative credit and envious skills base, no doubt remains an enormous production hub, and no one thinks this will suddenly screech to a halt. But with all these headwinds already being navigated, any further disruption would be most unwelcome.

Paul flew the flag for the incentive during a fireside chat at Qatar Film Committee’s Industry Days event in Doha earlier last week, claiming it will be “good for the world.” The Hollywood veteran may find it tough to convince Brits of his way of thinking.

Culture Is Driving New Interest in Travel for Ramadan

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Longer stays and a rise in cultural visits suggest Ramadan is becoming a more significant season in Abu Dhabi’s tourism calendar. The Department of Culture and Tourism - DCT Abu Dhabi now sees an opportunity to turn the holy month into a more deliberate travel proposition for both visitors and the global travel trade.

This sponsored content was created in collaboration with a Skift partner.

Ramadan has traditionally been viewed as a quieter month for travel in the Gulf. However, early data from DCT Abu Dhabi’s Ramadan 2026 season suggests that old assumptions about the holy month may be changing. The average length of stay across Abu Dhabi increased 3% year-on-year, while domestic hotel guests stayed 31% longer. Visits to cultural institutions more than doubled, rising from around 131,000 in Ramadan 2025 to over 291,000 in 2026.

Skift Studio spoke with Abdulla Yousuf, director of international operations at DCT Abu Dhabi, to explore how the emirate is making Ramadan a more intentional part of the visitor experience.

Skift Studio: How have international visitors and the travel trade traditionally viewed visiting Abu Dhabi during Ramadan, and how has that changed in recent years?

Abdulla Yousuf: Some international travelers and travel trade partners have historically viewed Ramadan as a period when activity may be lower across the region, especially regarding opening hours, dining options, and the availability of visitor experiences. We’ve collectively worked on changing that perception over the past few years. Abu Dhabi remains open during Ramadan. Our world-class hotels, cultural landmarks, theme parks, dining venues, and beaches remain open without interruption throughout the Holy Month.

What changes during Ramadan is not operational capacity, but the emirate’s rhythm. It transforms into a culturally rich season of community and reflection during the day, which gives way to an electric, festive atmosphere after sunset. The travel trade is increasingly recognizing that Ramadan is a value-added cultural window that cannot be experienced at any other time of the year.

Abu Dhabi saw an increase in the average length of stay during Ramadan 2026. What do you take from that change, and what factors do you think may have influenced it?

One likely contributing factor was the timing of Ramadan within Abu Dhabi’s cooler season, when comfortable temperatures make it easier to enjoy outdoor activities, such as waterfront promenades, kayaking in the mangroves, desert adventures, and visits to heritage sites.

At the same time, Ramadan reshapes the emirate into a distinctive day-to-night journey. Calm daytime exploration of beaches, resorts, and world-class attractions transitions into evenings centered around iftars, atmospheric night markets, community suhoors, and cultural activations like Layali Nights.

Extended opening hours make every day feel varied, immersive, and rewarding, naturally encouraging visitors to stay longer. This variety gives travel advisors and tour operators more opportunities to build itineraries that combine relaxation, culture, and outdoor experiences, helping position Abu Dhabi as a destination worth visiting during Ramadan.

What does the 2026 data tell you about how different visitor segments are using the destination?

The figures highlight two interesting trends. Visitors are spending longer in Abu Dhabi, and engagement with our cultural offering has increased significantly. The 31% year-on-year increase in length of stay among domestic hotel guests underscores the potential for Ramadan to drive longer stays in the domestic market. The increase in visits to our cultural institutions reinforces the importance of culture within the destination’s Ramadan offering.

The opportunity for our international travel trade partners is in communicating this diversity more effectively. Ramadan can appeal to visitors interested in culture, family experiences, hospitality, and outdoor activities, allowing partners to tailor itineraries to different traveler interests rather than treating the Holy Month as a single type of travel experience.

What role does culture play in Abu Dhabi’s Ramadan visitor experience?

Culture is central to the Ramadan experience in Abu Dhabi. The Holy Month offers visitors an opportunity not only to discover our cultural attractions but also to experience Emirati traditions, hospitality, and the importance of community firsthand.

Culture during the Holy Month is lived and shared. During the day, visitors explore landmarks such as Louvre Abu Dhabi, teamLab Phenomena Abu Dhabi, Zayed National Museum and Natural History Museum Abu Dhabi in Saadiyat Cultural District.

It becomes deeply communal at night, from heritage games at Al Jahili Fort and Ramadan at Qasr Al Hosn to shared iftars at Ezba Experiences, and late-night visits to Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque.

What makes Ramadan a great time to visit Abu Dhabi? What should visitors expect?

Visitors can expect a deeply authentic experience. First and foremost, Abu Dhabi is completely open for business. Every luxury resort, Michelin-recognized restaurant, shopping destination, and major attraction functions at full capacity with world-class hospitality.

After sunset, the emirate becomes remarkably vibrant. Dining evolves into a shared celebration, with iftars showcasing both global and Emirati flavors, followed by suhoors that invite people to slow down, connect, and enjoy live music, conversation, and warm hospitality late into the night. Above all, Emirati culture is rooted in generosity. International visitors are invited to sit at communal tables and build genuine connections with our community.

With Ramadan 2027 also falling during Abu Dhabi’s cooler season (February to March), visitors can combine these cultural experiences with time outdoors, whether exploring the Corniche, enjoying our beaches or discovering the desert.

My advice to anyone considering a visit is to embrace the distinct rhythm of the Holy Month. Ramadan offers a chance to discover Abu Dhabi at a slightly slower pace, while experiencing traditions and moments of community that make this time of year special.

What are you hearing from tour operators and travel advisors about selling Abu Dhabi during Ramadan? Where do knowledge gaps still exist, and how does the Experience Abu Dhabi Experts Ramadan module respond to them?

Some of the questions we encounter relate to practical considerations, such as opening hours, dining options, and the experiences available to visitors during the Holy Month. To address this, our International Operations team provides practical information and opportunities for first-hand experience of the destination. Our Experience Abu Dhabi Experts e-learning platform includes dedicated Ramadan training to help advisors better understand the visitor experience and communicate it to their clients.

We complement this with international trade engagement, including workshops in 23 markets, roadshows, and familiarisation trips that allow our partners to experience Abu Dhabi and its cultural offerings for themselves.

Ultimately, our role is to help the travel trade move beyond general awareness of Abu Dhabi and develop a deeper understanding of the destination. The better equipped our partners are to explain what visitors can experience during Ramadan, the more confidently they can recommend it and develop relevant itineraries.

How will DCT Abu Dhabi use learnings from Ramadan 2026 to shape the destination’s approach in 2027 and beyond?

The results from Ramadan 2026 have reinforced the importance of positioning the Holy Month as a distinctive part of Abu Dhabi’s year-round visitor offering. The increases in average length of stay and cultural engagement are encouraging and provide insights as we continue developing our approach for 2027 and beyond.

One priority is to continue working with our tourism and hospitality partners to showcase the range of experiences available during Ramadan, from cultural attractions and outdoor activities to evening dining and community celebrations.

Our wider ambition remains aligned with Abu Dhabi’s Tourism Strategy 2030: to attract visitors year-round, encourage longer stays, and strengthen the emirate’s position as a leading global destination. Ramadan plays an important role in that ambition by offering international visitors a culturally distinctive, uniquely Abu Dhabi experience.

To learn more about Abu Dhabi, visit visitabudhabi.ae

Mauricio Pochettino given £50m transfer budget at Man Utd - here’s who he should sign

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester United fans will be dismayed by reports that Mauricio Pochettino will have just £50million to improve his squad during the January transfer window.

A leaky defence has led to calls from ex-players such as Gary Neville for significant investment, but it appears Man Utd will try to do their business on a tight budget in January.

The second window of the season often leads to inflated prices, with clubs capitalising upon other teams’ struggles and desperate need for new players.

The defence is the priority, with Man Utd reportedly keen on finding a new left-back and centre-back, while Pochettino also wants a new striker.

Fikayo Tomori’s spell at AC Milan looks to be coming to a close at the six-year mark, with the England international caught in the transfer merry-go-round back in the summer.

Massimo Allegri froze the centre-back out of the AC Milan squad, only for the Italian giants to fail in recruiting a replacement. Even when drafted back into the first team, Tomori has found gametime hard to come by - waiting six Serie A games for his first appearance of the league season.

Currently sidelined with a hamstring issue, GOAL report that Tomori will not be offered a new contract when his current deal expires next summer. Despite being keen on a return to England, the 28-year-old rejected transfers to Fulham and Coventry City.

Tomori was said to be holding out on a move to Aston Villa or staying at AC Milan. The defender’s situation at San Siro could mean that Man Utd could reinforce their backline with an experienced player, that is also home-grown, on the cheap.

Milan were fielding bids worth around £15m in the summer, meaning a similar amount or even lower would likely be accepted in January.

To save even more money, Man Utd could make a pre-contract agreement with Tomori in January, paving the way for a free transfer next summer.

They cannot do that with Crystal Palace’s Tyrick Mitchell, despite the left-back also being out of contract at the end of the season. That is because English clubs cannot agree pre-contracts with players at other teams in England.

As such, Man Utd would have to open up their wallet and spend some cash on the 27-year-old, who has over 200 Premier League games under his belt making his debut during the 2019/20 season.

Mitchell has played his entire career for the Eagles, making a total of 260 appearances in blue and red. It has been reported that Palace hold a clause in Mitchell’s current deal to extend it for a further year, which preserves some of the left-back’s value.

Liverpool and Juventus have both previously been credited with an interest in Mitchell, as well as Man Utd. Previous reports have quoted transfer fees of £25m, which appears to be below the going rate for Premier League-proven left-backs.

Ivan Toney is another England international that Man Utd have been linked to. At first glance, the Red Devils’ £50mn budget would appear to nix any potential deal for the former Brentford man, Toney leaving London for £40million two seasons ago to sign for Al-Hilal in Saudi Arabia.

Not only would a transfer fee seemingly be a stretch, but also Toney’s inflated wages playing in the Middle East. However, recent reports from TEAMtalk claim that intermediaries are sounding out a potential loan move to Old Trafford, with the view of a future permanent transfer.

Such an arrangement would allow Man Utd to spend their money elsewhere, before making a decision on investing further cash into Toney after a loan period.

Still only 30 years old, Toney has recently been dropped from England duty. Harbouring ambitions of playing at Euro 2028, a move back to England may improve Toney’s chances of a call-up in two summers’ time.

A potential problem is that two former flames of Toney are interested in a reunion. His former Brentford boss Thomas Frank , now at Crystal Palace, is reportedly keen on bringing Toney back to London, while the striker’s old club Newcastle - backed by Saudi Arabia and managed by ex-Al-Hilal boss Mathias Jaissle - are also sniffing around a potential move.

Norse Atlantic Faces Fight for Survival as Cash Crisis Deepens

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Photo Credit: A Norse Atlantic 787 aircraft. Norse Atlantic Airways / Norse Atlantic Airways

Norse Atlantic Airways is fighting for its survival after warning on Wednesday that it needs fresh funding to continue operating. In a short stock exchange filing, the Norwegian low-cost, long-haul carrier described its financial situation as “critical.”

The company said it had explored various strategic alternatives with its advisors and was open to discussions over a potential takeover.

More immediately, Norse is in talks with selected parties to secure short-term financing. However, the airline warned that “there can be no assurances that such discussions will result in a viable solution.”

Shares in Norse plunged to an all-time low following the announcement, down more than 70% as of 1 p.m. Oslo-time on Wednesday. The company did not disclose how much money it needs or how long it can continue operating without additional funding.

Norse Atlantic Airways told the market on Wednesday that its financial situation is "critical" and that it needs fresh funding to keep operating, sending its shares down more than 70% to an all-time low. The Norwegian low-cost, long-haul carrier said it is in talks with selected parties over short-term financing and is open to discussions about a takeover, but warned there is no assurance the talks will produce a viable solution. It did not disclose how much money it needs or how long it can operate without it. The warning comes despite strong commercial indicators: planes were almost 95% full in October and unit revenues on its own network rose 19% year over year, though on a much smaller schedule of 199 flights versus 355 a year earlier. Norse has been shifting its 12 Boeing 787s toward ACMI work for other carriers — most recently a deal with Pakistan International Airlines covering two 787-9s on UK-Pakistan flights — alongside a rights issue in June, a $52 million financing agreement in August, and Project Falcon, a cost program targeting $50 million in annual savings from 2027. The warning lands at the start of winter, the weakest period for European leisure-focused airlines.

The Lord Of The Rings: The Rings Of Power Season 3's Shocking Death Leaves Its Biggest Question Unanswered

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In the land of Mordor, where the spoilers lie. This article covers major plot details from the Season 3 finale of "The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power."

How's that for a red herring? "The Rings of Power" Season 3 seemed to confirm one of the biggest fan-theories around: that Theo (Tyroe Muhafidin), the sulky Southlander with an odd tendency to gravitate towards darkness, was well on his way to becoming one of the fearsome Nazgnagôl. The presumption among many has been that he wouldn't merely be a Ring Servant of Sauron (Charlie Vickers), in fact, but the greatest of them all, i.e. the Witch-king. The finale, however, decided to introduce a late contender for that dubious crown. Ever an agent of chaos, Sauron gifts one of his rings to the Dark Wizard Pallando (Ciarán Hinds) and even dubs him as "the greatest of the Nine — a Witch-king no man can slay." Case closed, right? Wrong!

Late in the episode, Pallando successfully turns away from the dark side (thanks to the good-natured urgings of Daniel Weyman's Gandalf, of course) and rejects all the seductive offers that come with Sauron's ring ... but sacrifices his life to save his "old friend." This shocking death resolves one storyline while adding all sorts of question marks to another. As Gandalf makes his way westward with merry ol' Tom Bombadil (Rory Kinnear), that appears to close the book on the action in Rhûn. At the same time, however, we're still left wondering about where, exactly, Theo fits into all this mess. Is he merely meant to be one of the lower-ranked members of the Ringwraiths, doomed to forever take orders (and abuse) from his Dark Lord? Or is he still a Witch-king in the making?

Did anyone expect Pallando's arc to end quite like this? The former Blue Wizard who murdered his own friend Alatar may not have seemed like the most obvious candidate for redemption, but Gandalf manages to pull him back from the brink just in time to help save the day and lessen Sauron's forces significantly. Unfortunately, he succumbs to his wound inflicted by the Nazgnagôl known as Khamûl (Zubin Varla) and passes away dramatically — complete with the stunning visual of his spirit actually departing his body and making its way west, well beyond the sight of mere mortals. Despite seemingly paralleling the storyline of the white wizard Saruman's downfall (played by Christopher Lee in Peter Jackson's Middle-earth films), Pallando rewrites his own ending.

It puts a fitting and satisfying enough bow on a subplot that had finally run out of juice. It's just not that the empty wastelands of Rhûn are visually boring, the action far too removed from the rest of Middle-earth to feel all that urgent (no matter how many hundreds of thousands of enemies live there), and the tone just a little too close to "Harry Potter" for our liking. It's all of that, plus the fact that this prolonged showdown with Pallando prevented Gandalf from ever interacting with the rest of our main heroes. It's long past time for our favorite wizard to cross paths with the likes of Galadriel (Morfydd Clark), Elrond (Robert Aramayo), and Gil-galad (Benjamin Walker) and finally feel like an integral member of this story, and now, he's finally free to do so.

Either way, RIP Pallando, the most gloriously bearded wizard in all of Middle-earth (and with an attitude to match).

Speaking of subplots without much juice, well, there's no nice way to say this: Theo, we are officially done with your nonsense. Barely one episode after coming oh-so-close to rejecting Sauron's offer and proving that the Men of Middle-earth still have some genuine fight left in them, our unconvincing antagonist begins the finale right back by Sauron's side after failing to destroy his ring. Still, even that wouldn't be so bad if we at least received some clarity regarding this whole Witch-king business. With Pallando out of the picture and Khamûl seemingly dead (although, in fairness, we never see his body after he's thrown out of Sauron's tower of Barad-dûr), well, Theo's pretty much the leading contender by default, no?

Well, it's more complicated than that. Theo's first real interaction with Sauron in the finale is as the deliverer of bad news, informing his Dark Lord that half their army has deserted following Pallando's death and (presumably) the breaking of the spell he had cast upon them. And, as you can imagine, this doesn't exactly go well for him as Sauron grabs him by the throat and demands to know why he couldn't stop them.

But perhaps their last scene together holds some clues. While preparing for battle with the combined force of Númenor and the Elves, Sauron slips off his One Ring and gives himself up to his enemies. It's unlikely he'd keep carrying that on his person, so did he perhaps entrust his most "precious" creation to Theo? Is this all part of an evil plan that will see the Southlander ascend to the ultimate position of authority among the Ringwraiths? We'll sadly have to wait for Season 4.

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are leaning towards allowing Lisandro Martinez to leave the club next summer despite already lacking depth at the centre-back position. United are in troubled times, currently sat in 19th position and desperately hoping new boss Mauricio Pochettino can turn things around quickly.

Liverpool thrashed United 4-0 in Pochettino's first game, although the Red Devils were not helped by a red card awarded to Leny Yoro that was later removed on appeal. But United did not play well in any case, and they certainly didn't defend well amid ongoing issues at the back.

Matthijs de Ligt lacks match fitness and sharpness, while Yoro is still learning and Martinez has been in poor form this season. Harry Maguire, meanwhile, cannot get a look in.

United have some interesting contract decisions to make in the summer window, and one of the biggest is Martinez, who initially arrived at the club on a £56.7million deal. Two long-term ACL injuries have followed, and it's fair to say the Argentine hasn't lived up to expectations. He didn't help himself when he spoke out against the club earlier this summer amid struggles under Michael Carrick.

Martinez is out of contract in the summer, but United do have an option to extend the deal by one year, albeit they are only likely to activate that if both they and the player want to continue their relationship.

And according to GIVEMESPORT's Ben Jacobs, United are not likely to extend the deal as things stand. He said on the Market Madness podcast: “I think the Lisandro Martinez direction of travel is a departure, and there is the option to extend by one year. But my gut says that Manchester United, even if they can’t get defenders in the winter, will add further in the summer because they don’t just need one defender; they need a centre-back, they need a left-back, you could say they need a right-back.

"And in getting these full-backs, they might even get a full-back that’s a centre-back. So you might have centre-back, a wing-back that can play centre-back, and you’ve obviously still got young players like Ayden Heaven and Leny Yoro.

So this feels to me like Martinez, on form and on age, and even to a degree on wage, although he’s not like top five at Manchester United, the beginning of the end.

"And even if Man United extend the contract, that might just be a protection so they can still sell him.”

There have been reports that Pochettino wants to keep Martinez, with the defender certainly having the ability with the ball at his feet that the new United boss likes in his centre-backs, and there is an obvious Argentine connection. But there is also a business reality to the contract situation, and that will mean club higher ups like Jacon Wilcox will get a big say.

With such an accomplished life and so many lives touched, it's not surprising that there was an outpouring of love from Mrs. Ford online after the news broke. Many teams, media members, former players, and even fans shared their interactions and thoughts about her.

Ford was one of the few female figureheads leading an NFL team. So a few other women in leadership roles around the NFL talked about her being a trailblazer in the business.

"I am deeply saddened to learn of the passing of Martha Firestone Ford. Mrs. Ford was a true matriarch of the National Football League and an extraordinary pillar of the Detroit community for more than six decades.

Having stepped into the role of principal owner following the loss of her beloved husband, I deeply admired the grace, strength, and unwavering dedication with which she led the Detroit Lions. She paved the way for women in sports ownership and set an enduring standard for leadership, philanthropy, and devotion to family.

On behalf of the entire New Orleans Saints organization, I extend my heartfelt prayers and deepest condolences to Sheila, Martha, Bill, Elizabeth, and the entire Ford family, as well as the Detroit Lions organization during this difficult time."

U.S. proposes new tariffs on 14 trading partners, including China and the EU

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The U.S. has proposed new tariffs on 14 partners including China, the European Union, Japan and India, as President Donald Trump doubles down on levies, despite a large chunk of his previous duties being struck down by the Supreme Court.

The targeted economies create, sustain, or contribute to structural excess capacity and production in manufacturing sectors which burden or restrict American commerce, a senior U.S. official said.

The 14 partners have been notified of these actions, with neither country's tariffs exceeding rates announced as part of framework deals on reciprocal trade, the official added.

The new tariffs will not stack on top of existing duties, except for China, which faces a 20% tariff on top of the 25% imposed in 2018 over forced technology transfers.

The new duties on the world's second-largest economy could jump to 40% if it does not hold up its end of the trade truce agreed in Busan, South Korea, in Oct. 2025, after which tariffs on Chinese exports to the U.S. were slashed to 47%.

The administration says the tariffs target what it calls "overcapacity" — excess industrial production, often supported by government subsidies, that allows cheaper goods to flood global markets and undercut U.S. producers.

"The so-called 'overcapacity' does not really exist," China's foreign ministry said on Wednesday before the official reports were released by the U.S. Trade Representative, according to Reuters.

The new levies are being imposed under Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974, which allows Washington to counter foreign trade practices deemed unfair, discriminatory, or burdensome to U.S. commerce.

CNBC earlier reported that the new tariffs would be "country specific," rather than a blanket rate, citing a source familiar with the matter.

Tariffs have been a signature of the Trump administration since the president unveiled sweeping "Liberation Day" duties in April 2025 before pausing them after a sour reaction from bond markets.

In February, the Supreme Court struck down tariffs that Trump had enacted under the International Emergency Economic Powers Act.

Trump used Section 122 of the Trade Act of 1974 to impose a 10% global tariff after the court's decision, before replacing them with levies of up to 12.5% on 60 economies under Section 301, alleging forced labor practices.
